  <td title='t1.1.27b13'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>諸佛金剛體，<br/>
    皆亦歸無常；<br/>
    速滅如少雪，<br/>
    其餘復何冀？」</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The vajra bodies of buddhas<br/>
    All return to impermanence, too.<br/>
    They cease quickly like a little snow;<br/>
    Why hope for anything else to remain?”</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27b15'></td>
  <td class='subheading'>39. Paying Last Respects</td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27b15'>佛般涅槃已。時，諸比丘悲慟殞絕，自投於地，宛轉號咷，不能自勝，歔欷而言：「如來滅度，何其<!-- [11] -->駛哉！世尊滅度，何其疾哉！大法淪翳，何其速哉！群生長衰，世間眼滅。</td>
    <td id='p486'>486. The Buddha had parinirvāṇa-ed. The monks lamented his death. They threw themselves to the ground, twisting and crying loudly. They couldn’t control themselves. Sobbing, they said, “How quick the Tathāgata’s extinguishment was! How swift the Bhagavān’s extinguishment was! How fast the great Dharma falls into obscurity! The multitude of beings will decline for a long time! The world will go blind!”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27b18'>「譬如大樹根拔，枝條摧折。又如斬蛇，宛轉迴遑，莫知所<!-- [12] -->奉。」時，諸比丘亦復如是，悲慟殞絕，自投於地，宛轉號咷，不能自勝，歔欷而言：「如來滅度，何其<!-- [＊] -->駛哉！世尊滅度，何其疾哉！大法淪翳，何其速哉！群生長衰，世間眼滅。」</td>
    <td id='p487'>487. They were like a large tree pulled up by the roots with its branches chopped off. They were like a cut snake that twists, turns, and writhes in a frenzy, not knowing where to go. The monks were likewise as they lamented his death. They threw themselves to the ground, twisting and crying loudly. They couldn’t control themselves. Sobbing, they said, “How quick the Tathāgata’s extinguishment was! How swift the Bhagavān’s extinguishment was! How fast the great Dharma falls into obscurity! The multitude of beings will decline for a long time! The world will go blind!”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27b23'>爾時，長老阿那律告諸比丘：「止！止！勿悲，諸天在上，儻有恠責。」</td>
    <td id='p488'>488. The elder Aniruddha then told the monks, “Stop, stop! Don’t cry! There are gods above us. They might consider it strange and fault you for it!”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27b24'>時，諸比丘問阿那律：「上有幾天？」</td>
    <td id='p489'>489. The monks asked Aniruddha, “How many gods are there above us?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27b25'>阿那律言：「充滿虛空，豈可計量？皆於空中徘徊騷擾，悲號躃踊，埀淚而言：『如來滅度，何其<!-- [＊] -->駛哉？世尊滅度，何其疾哉？大法淪翳，何其速哉？群生長衰，世間眼滅。</td>
    <td id='p490'>490. Aniruddha said, “They fill the sky. Who could count them all? They are roaming around the sky, distraught, crying, and beating their breasts. With tears streaming, they say, ‘How quick the Tathāgata’s extinguishment was! How swift the Bhagavān’s extinguishment was! How fast the great Dharma falls into obscurity! The multitude of beings will decline for a long time! The world will go blind!’</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27b29'>譬如大樹根拔，枝條摧折，又如斬蛇，宛轉迴遑，莫知所<!-- [＊] -->奉。』是時，諸天亦復如是，皆於空中徘徊騷擾，悲號躃踊，埀淚而言：『如來滅度，何其<!-- [＊] -->駛哉！世尊滅度，何其疾哉！大法淪翳，何其速哉！群生長衰，世間眼滅。』」</td>
    <td id='p491'>491. “They’re like a large tree pulled up by the roots with its branches chopped off. They’re like a cut snake that twists, turns, and writhes in a frenzy, not knowing where to go. The gods are likewise. They’re roaming around the sky, distraught, crying, and beating their breasts. With tears streaming, they say, ‘How quick the Tathāgata’s extinguishment was! How swift the Bhagavān’s extinguishment was! How fast the great Dharma falls into obscurity! The multitude of beings will decline for a long time! The world will go blind!’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c5'>時，諸比丘竟夜達曉，講法語已，阿那律告阿難言：「汝可入城，語諸末羅：『佛已滅度，所欲施作，宜及時為。』」</td>
    <td id='p492'>492. The monks discussed the teaching throughout the night until daybreak. Aniruddha told Ānanda, “Perhaps you should go to the city and tell the Mallas, ‘The Buddha has been extinguished. If you’d like to give offerings, now would be a good time to do it.’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c7'>是時，阿難即起，禮佛足已，將一比丘，涕泣入城，遙見五百末羅以少因緣，集在一處。諸末羅見阿難來，皆起奉迎，禮足而立，白阿難言：「今來何早？」</td>
    <td id='p493'>493. Ānanda got up, bowed at the Buddha’s feet, and then led another monk to the city, crying as they went. They saw a group of 500 Mallas gathered in one place for some minor reason. The Mallas saw Ānanda coming and they got up to watch him approached. After bowing at his feet and standing aside, they said to Ānanda, “Why have you come this morning?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c10'> 阿難答言：「我今為欲饒益汝故，晨來至此。汝等當知：如來昨夜已取滅度，汝欲施作，宜及時為。」</td>
    <td id='p494'>494. Ānanda replied, “Now, I’ve come here to benefit all of you. You should know that the Tathāgata was extinguished last night. If you’d like to give offerings, now is a good time to do it.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c13'>時，諸末羅聞是語已，莫不悲慟，捫淚而言：「一何駛哉！佛般涅槃。一何疾哉！世間眼滅。」</td>
    <td id='p495'>495. After hearing him say this, all the Mallas lamented loudly. Wiping their tears, they said, “The Buddha parinirvāṇa-ed so fast! The world went blind so swiftly!”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c15'>阿難報曰：「止！止！諸君勿為悲泣，欲使有為不變易者，無有是處。佛已先說：『生者有死，合會有離；一切恩愛，無常存者。』」</td>
    <td id='p496'>496. Ānanda responded, “Stop, stop! Gentlemen, don’t cry! The desire for this conditioned life to not change is impossible. As the Buddha said before, ‘What’s born has its death, and what comes together also falls apart. All that we love is impermanent.’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c17'>時，諸末羅各相謂言：「宜各還歸，辦諸香花及眾伎樂，速詣雙樹，供養舍利。竟一日已，以佛舍利置於牀上，使末羅童子舉牀四角，<!-- [13] -->擎持幡蓋，燒香散華，伎樂供養，入東城門，遍諸里巷，使國<!-- [14] -->人民皆得供養；然後出西城門，詣高顯處而闍維之。」</td>
    <td id='p497'>497. The Mallas then said to each other, “We each should return home, pick out fragrant flowers and musical instruments, and then go quickly to that pair of trees to give offerings to the Buddha’s remains! After a day, we’ll put his remains on a bed. Young Malla men will carry the bed at each corner. We’ll carry banners and parasols, burn incense, scatter flowers, and play music as offerings. We’ll enter the city’s east gate and pass through neighborhood after neighborhood so that people can give offerings. Afterward, we’ll exit through the west gate, take the bed to an elevated place, and cremate him there.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c23'>時，諸末羅作此論已，各自還家，供辦香華及眾伎樂，詣雙樹間，供養舍利。竟一日已，以佛舍利置於牀上，諸末羅等<!-- [16] -->眾來舉<!-- [17] -->牀，皆不能勝。</td>
    <td id='p498'>498. Once they had had this discussion, the Mallas each returned to their homes and picked out fragrant flowers and musical instruments for their offerings. They then went to that place between a pair of trees to give offerings to the Buddha’s remains. After a day, his remains were placed on a bed, but when the Mallas [attempted to] lift the bed, none of them could manage it.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c26'>時，阿那律語諸末羅：「汝等且止！勿空疲勞，今者諸天欲來舉牀。」</td>
    <td id='p499'>499. Aniruddha then said to the Mallas, “Stop, all of you! Don’t exhaust yourselves needlessly. The gods are going to lift the bed.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c28'>諸末羅曰：「天<!-- [18] -->以何意，欲舉此牀？」</td>
    <td id='p500'>500. The Mallas asked, “What do the gods have in mind? They’re going to lift this bed?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.27c28'>阿那律曰：「汝等欲以香花伎樂供養舍利，竟一日已，以佛舍利置於牀上，使末羅童子舉牀四角，擎持幡蓋，燒香散花，伎樂供養，入東城門，遍諸里巷，使國人民皆得供養；然後出西城門，詣高顯處而闍維之。</td>
    <td id='p501'>501. Aniruddha said, “You were going to give offerings of fragrant flowers and music to the Buddha’s remains. After a day, you would place the remains on a bed and have young Malla men carry it at each corner. You would carry banners and parasols, burn incense, scatter flowers, and play music as offerings. You then would enter the city’s east gate and pass through neighborhood after neighborhood so that people can make offerings. Afterward, you would exit through the west gate, take the bed to an elevated place, and cremate him there.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a4'>而諸天意欲留舍利七日之中，香花伎樂，禮敬供養；然後以佛舍利置於牀上，使末羅童子舉牀四角，擎持幡蓋，散花燒香，作眾伎樂，供養舍利。入東城門，遍諸里巷，使國人民皆得供養；然後出城北門，渡凞連禪河，到天冠寺而闍維之。是上天意，使牀不動。」</td>
    <td id='p502'>502. “But the gods wish to leave his remains here for seven days to give offerings of incense, flowers, and music and to pay our respects. Afterward, the Buddha’s remains will be placed on a bed. Young Malla men will carry the bed at each corner. You’ll carry banners and parasols, scatter flowers, burn incense, and play music as offerings to his remains. You’ll enter the city’s east gate and go through neighborhood after neighborhood so that people can give offerings. Afterward, you’ll exit through the north gate, ford the Nairañjanā River, and go to the Heavenly Crown Temple. He’ll be cremated there. This is what gods above want, so they are not allowing the bed to be moved.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a10'>末羅曰：「諾！快哉斯言！隨諸天意。」</td>
    <td id='p503'>503. The Mallas said, “Oh, that sounds good! We’ll follow the wishes of the gods.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a11'>時，諸末羅自相謂言：「我等宜先入城，街里<!-- [1] -->街里，平治道路，掃灑燒香，還來至此，於七日中供養舍利。」</td>
    <td id='p504'>504. The Mallas then said to each other, “We should go to the city first and put the streets in the neighborhoods in order. We’ll sweep them and burn incense. Then, we can come back here to give offerings to his remains for seven days.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a14'>時，諸末羅即共入城，街里<!-- [＊] -->街里，平治道路，掃灑燒香，訖已出城，於雙樹間，以香花伎樂供養舍利。訖七日已，時日向暮，舉佛舍利置於牀上，末羅童子<!-- [2] -->奉舉四角，擎持幡蓋，燒香散花，作眾伎樂，前後導從，安詳而行。</td>
    <td id='p505'>505. The Mallas then went together to the city and put the streets in order, going from neighborhood to neighborhood. They swept up refuse and burned incense. When they were finished, they left the city and returned to the place between a pair of trees. They gave offerings of incense, flowers, and music to the Buddha’s remains. When the sun set after seven days had passed, they placed the Buddha’s remains on a bed. Young Malla men carried it at each corner. A crowd of Mallas carried banners and parasols, burned incense, scattered flowers, and played music. They followed the bed bearers in front and back, walking peacefully as they went.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a19'>時，忉利諸天以文陀羅花、優鉢羅花、<!-- [＊] -->波頭摩花、拘物頭花、分陀利花、天末栴檀散舍利上，充滿街路。諸天作樂，鬼神歌詠。</td>
    <td id='p506'>506. The Trāyastriṃśa gods scattered mandara flowers, utpala flowers, padma flowers, kumuda flowers, puṇḍarīka flowers, and heavenly sandalwood powder onto the Buddha’s remains, such that it filled up the roadway. The gods played music, and yakṣa spirits sang songs.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a21'>時，諸末羅自相謂言：「且置人樂，請設天樂供養舍利。」</td>
    <td id='p507'>507. The Mallas said to each other, “Let’s set aside our human music in favor of the music the gods are playing as offerings to the Buddha’s remains.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a23'>於是，末羅奉牀漸進，入東城門，止諸街巷，燒香散花，伎樂供養。時，有路夷末羅女篤信佛道，手擎金花，大如車輪，供養舍利。</td>
    <td id='p508'>508. The Mallas carrying the bed made their way to the city and entered the east gate. They stopped at intersections, where they burned incense, scattered flowers, and played music as offerings. There was then the Malla woman *Royī who was a believer in the Buddha’s path. She carried a gold flower in her hand as large as a cartwheel, which she gave as an offering to his remains.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a25'>時，有一老母舉聲讚曰：「此諸末羅為得大利，如來末後於此滅度，舉國士民快得供養。」</td>
    <td id='p509'>509. There was an elderly grandmother who raised her voice in praise, “These Mallas will get a great reward! For the whole nation delights in giving the final offerings to the Tathāgata after his extinguishment!”</td>
  </tr>
<tr>
  <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a28'></td>
  <td class='subheading'>40. The Buddha’s Funeral</td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28a28'>時，諸末羅設供養已，出城北門，渡凞連禪河，到天冠寺，置牀於地，告阿難曰：「我等當復<!-- [3] -->以何供養？」</td>
    <td id='p511'>511. After the Mallas had given their offerings, they left through the north gate, forded the Nairañjanā River, and went to Heavenly Crown Temple. They placed the bed on the ground and told Ānanda, “What else should we give as an offering?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b1'>阿難報曰：「我親從佛聞，親受佛教，欲<!-- [4] -->葬舍利者，當如轉輪聖王葬法。」</td>
    <td id='p512'>512. Ānanda responded, “I personally heard this from the Buddha, personally received this instruction from the Buddha: When we inter his remains, it should be as a noble wheel-turning king is interred.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b3'>又問阿難：「轉輪聖王葬法云何？」</td>
    <td id='p513'>513. Again, they asked Ānanda, “How is a noble wheel-turning king interred?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b3'>答曰：「聖王葬法，先以香湯洗浴其身，以新劫貝周遍纏身，五百張<!-- [＊] -->疊次如纏之，內身金棺，灌以麻油畢，舉金棺置於第二大鐵槨中，栴檀香槨次重於外，積眾名香，厚衣其上而闍維之。<!-- [5] -->收拾舍利，於四衢道起立塔廟，表剎懸繒，使國行人皆見<!-- [6] -->王塔，思慕正化，多所饒益。</td>
    <td id='p514'>514. He replied, “‘This is the way of interring a noble [wheel-turning] king: First, his body is bathed in fragrant water. He’s wrapped all around with fresh cotton. Next, he’s wrapped in 500 layers of cloth. His body is placed in a gold coffin and sesame oil is poured onto it. The gold coffin is lifted and placed in a second larger iron coffin. Sandalwood incense is next layered outside of that coffin. Firewood of many fragrances is piled on top of fine robes, and then he is cremated. When it’s done, his remains are placed in a shrine built at a crossroads. It’s exterior is hung with silks, and people from the country travel to see the Dharma king’s shrine. They think longingly about the correct teaching that benefited many people.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b10'>『阿難，汝欲葬我，先以香湯洗浴，用新劫貝周匝纏身，以五百張<!-- [＊] -->疊次如纏之。內身金棺，灌以麻油畢，舉金棺置於第二大鐵槨中，栴檀香槨次重於外，積眾名香，厚衣其上而闍維之。收撿舍利，於四衢道起立塔廟，表剎懸繒，使諸行人皆見佛塔，思慕如來法王道化，生獲福利，死得上天，除得道者。』」</td>
    <td id='p515'>515. “‘Ānanda, when you inter me, first bathe my body in fragrant water and then wrap it all around with fresh cotton. Wrap it in 500 layers of cloth, place my body inside a gold coffin and pour sesame oil on it. Lift the gold coffin and place it in a second larger iron coffin. Next, layer sandalwood incense on the outside of that coffin. Pile firewood of many fragrances on top of fine robes, and then cremate me. When it’s done, place my remains in a shrine built at a crossroads. Hang its exterior with silks, and have people from the country travel to see the Buddha’s shrine. They’ll think longingly about the Tathāgata, the Dharma king, and his awakened teaching. While they’re alive, they will get merits and rewards, and they’ll be born up in heaven when they die, aside from those who attain awakening.’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b17'>時，諸末羅各相謂言：「我等還城，供辦葬具、香花、劫貝、棺槨、香油及與白疊。」</td>
    <td id='p516'>516. The Mallas then said to each other, “Let’s go back to the city and fetch the supplies for his interment, such as incense, flowers, cotton, coffins, fragrant oil, and white cloth.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b19'>時，諸末羅即共入城，供辦葬具已，還到天冠寺，以淨香湯洗浴佛身，以新劫貝周匝纏身，五百張<!-- [＊] -->疊次如纏之。內身金棺，灌以香油，<!-- [＊] -->奉舉金棺置於第二大鐵槨中，栴檀木槨重衣其外，以眾名香而𧂐其上。</td>
    <td id='p517'>517. The Mallas then went to the city together, arranged the supplies for the interment, and returned to Heavenly Crown Temple. They bathed the Buddha’s body in fragrant water and then wrapped it all around with fresh cotton. They wrapped it in 500 layers of cloth, placed his body inside a gold coffin, and poured fragrant oil on it. They lifted the gold coffin and placed it in a second larger iron coffin. Next, they layered sandalwood on the outside of that coffin. They piled firewood of many fragrances on top of it.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b24'>時，有末羅大臣名曰路夷，執大炬火，欲燃佛<!-- [7] -->積，而火不燃。又有<!-- [8] -->大末羅次前燃[9a]其積，[9b]火又不燃。</td>
    <td id='p518'>518. The great minister of the Mallas named *Royī carried a large torch and tried to light the Buddha’s firewood, but the fire wouldn’t start. Other prominent Mallas came forward to light the wood, but again the fire wouldn’t start.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b26'>時，阿那律語諸末羅言：「止！止！諸賢！非汝所能，火滅不燃，是諸天意。」</td>
    <td id='p519'>519. Aniruddha then said to the Mallas, “Stop, stop! Gentlemen, you won’t be able to light it. The fire goes out and doesn’t burn because that’s what the gods want.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b28'>末羅又問：「諸天何故使火不燃？」</td>
    <td id='p520'>520. The Mallas asked, “Why are the gods preventing the fire from lighting?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28b28'>阿那律言：「天以大迦葉將五百弟子從波<!-- [11] -->婆國來，今在半道，及未闍維，欲見佛身，天知其意，故<!-- [12] -->使火不燃。」</td>
    <td id='p521'>521. Aniruddha said, “The gods would like Mahākāśyapa to bring 500 disciples from Pāvā. At the moment, they are halfway here on the road. Don’t cremate him yet. They want to look at the Buddha’s body. The gods know his wish, so they are preventing the fire from lighting.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c2'>末羅又言：「願遂此意！」</td>
    <td id='p522'>522. The Mallas again said, “Please let them get what they want.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c2'>爾時，大迦葉將五百弟子從波<!-- [＊] -->婆國來，在道而行，遇一尼乾子手執<!-- [＊] -->文陀羅花。時，大迦葉遙見尼乾子，就往問言：「汝從何來？」</td>
    <td id='p523'>523. Mahākāśyapa then was leading those 500 disciples from Pāvā. As they walked on the road, they encountered a Nirgrantha carrying a mandara flower in his hand. When he saw the Nirgrantha, Mahākāśyapa stopped him and asked, “Where are you coming from?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c5'>報言：「吾從拘尸城來。」</td>
    <td id='p524'>524. He replied, “I’m coming from Kuśinagara.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c6'>迦葉又言：「汝知我師乎？」</td>
    <td id='p525'>525. Kāśyapa again asked, “Do you know about our teacher?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c6'>答曰：「知。」</td>
    <td id='p526'>526. “I do.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c7'>又問：「我師存耶？」</td>
    <td id='p527'>527. “Do you know where he is?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c7'>答曰：「滅度已來，已經七日，吾從彼來，得此天華。」</td>
    <td id='p528'>528. “It’s been seven days since his extinguishment. I am going there to give this heavenly flower.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c8'>迦葉聞之，悵然不悅。時，五百比丘聞佛滅度，皆大悲泣，宛轉號咷，不能自勝，捫淚而言：「如來滅度，何其<!-- [＊] -->駛哉！世尊滅度，何其疾哉！大法淪翳，何其速哉！群生長衰，世間眼滅。譬如大樹根拔，枝條摧折，又如斬蛇，宛轉迴遑，莫知所<!-- [＊] -->奉。」</td>
    <td id='p529'>529. When Kāśyapa heard this, he became depressed and unhappy. When the other 500 monks heard the Buddha was extinguished, they cried loudly, twisting, turning, and wailing. They couldn’t control themselves. Wiping their tears, they said, “How quick the Tathāgata’s extinguishment was! How swift the Bhagavān’s extinguishment was! How fast the great Dharma falls into obscurity! The multitude of beings will decline for a long time! The world will go blind!” They were like a large tree pulled up by the roots with its branches chopped off. They were like a cut snake that twists, turns, and writhes in a frenzy, not knowing where to go.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c13'>時，彼眾中有釋種子，字<!-- [14] -->拔難陀，止諸比丘言：「汝等勿憂，世尊滅度，我得自在。彼<!-- [15] -->者常言：『當應行是，不應行是。』自今已後，隨我所為。」</td>
    <td id='p530'>530. There was a Śākya man in his assembly named Upananda. He stopped the monks by saying, “Don’t be sad! The Bhagavān is extinguished. We’re free now! He was always saying, ‘You must do this; you mustn’t do that.’ From now on, we can do as we like.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c16'>迦葉聞已，悵然不悅，告諸比丘曰：「速嚴衣鉢，時詣雙樹，及未闍維，可得見佛。」</td>
    <td id='p531'>531. When Kāśyapa heard that, he was depressed and unhappy. He told the monks, “Quickly, get your robes and bowls. We’ll need to get to that pair of trees before he is cremated if we are to see the Buddha.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c18'>時，諸比丘聞大迦葉語已，即從座起，侍從迦葉，詣拘尸城，渡<!-- [16] -->尼連禪河水，到天冠寺，至阿難所。問訊已，一面住，語阿難言：「我等欲一面覲舍利，及未闍維，寧可見不？」</td>
    <td id='p532'>532. When they heard what he said, the monks rose from their seats and followed Kāśyapa as he went to Kuśinagara. They forded the Nairañjanā River, arrived at Heavenly Crown Temple, and went to Ānanda. After exchanging greetings, they stood to one side. Kāśyapa said to Ānanda, “We would like to see the remains before he is cremated. Would it be possible to see him?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c22'>阿難答言：「雖未闍維，難復可見。所以然者？佛身既<!-- [18] -->洗以香湯纏以劫貝，五百張<!-- [＊] -->疊次如纏之。藏於金棺，置鐵槨中，栴檀香槨重衣其外，以為佛身難復可覩。」</td>
    <td id='p533'>533. Ānanda answered, “Although he hasn’t been cremated yet, it’ll be difficult to see him. Why is that? The Buddha’s body was bathed in fragrant water and then wrapped all around with fresh cotton. They wrapped it in 500 layers of cloth and placed his body inside a gold coffin, which was placed in an iron coffin. They layered sandalwood on the outside of that coffin. It’ll be difficult to view the Buddha’s body because of that.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c26'>迦葉請至三，阿難答如初：「以為佛身難復得見。」</td>
    <td id='p534'>534. Kāśyapa made his request three times, and Ānanda answered as he did the first time: “… It will be difficult to view the Buddha’s body because of that.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.28c27'>時，大迦葉<!-- [19] -->適<!-- [20] -->向香𧂐，於時佛身從重<!-- [21] -->槨內雙出兩足，足有異色，迦葉見已，怪問阿難：「佛身金色，<!-- [22] -->足何故異？」</td>
    <td id='p535'>535. Mahākāśyapa went over to the fragrant firewood. He uncovered the Buddha’s feet inside the double casket, and they had changed color. Seeing that, Kāśyapa was shocked. He asked Ānanda, “The Buddha’s body is golden. Why have his feet changed?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29a1'>阿難報曰：「向者，有一老母悲哀而前手撫佛足，淚墮其上，故色異耳。」</td>
    <td id='p536'>536. Ānanda replied, “There was an elderly mother earlier who was lamenting and touched the Buddha’s feet with her hand, and her tears were on them. That’s the only reason they changed color.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29a2'>迦葉聞已，又大不悅，即向香𧂐，禮佛舍利。時，四部眾及上諸天同時俱禮，於是佛足忽然不現。</td>
    <td id='p537'>537. Hearing this, Kāśyapa was even more unhappy. He went to the fragrant firewood and bowed to the Buddha’s remains. The fourfold assembly and the gods above also bowed together at the same time. Thereupon, the Buddha’s feet suddenly disappeared.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29a5'>時，大迦葉繞𧂐三匝，而作頌曰：</td>
    <td id='p538'>538. Mahākāśyapa circled the firewood three times and composed these verses:</td>
  </tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a6'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>「諸佛無等等，<br/>
    聖智不可稱；<br/>
    無等之聖智，<br/>
    我今稽首禮。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>“Buddhas are unparalleled,<br/>
    Their noble wisdom is inestimable.<br/>
    To the unequalled noble wisdom,<br/>
    I now bow my head.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a8'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>無等等沙門，<br/>
    最上無瑕穢；<br/>
    牟尼絕愛枝，<br/>
    大仙天人尊；<br/>
    人中第一雄，<br/>
    我今𥡳首禮。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The unparalleled ascetic<br/>
    Was supreme and flawless.<br/>
    The Muni who broke the branch of craving,<br/>
    That great sage is honored by gods and humans.<br/>
    To the supreme hero of humans,<br/>
    I now bow my head.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a11'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>苦行無等侶，<br/>
    離著而教人；<br/>
    無染無垢塵，<br/>
    稽首無上尊。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>His ascetic practice had no counterpart,<br/>
    He taught people to part with attachment.<br/>
    He was unstained and undirtied;<br/>
    I bow my head to the Unsurpassed Sage.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a13'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>三垢垢已盡，<br/>
    樂於空寂行；<br/>
    無二無疇匹，<br/>
    𥡳首十力尊。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The three defilements being ended,<br/>
    He was happy practicing in tranquility.<br/>
    To the Peerless and Companionless,<br/>
    I bow my head to the Ten-Powered Sage.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a15'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>善逝為最上，<br/>
    二足尊中尊；<br/>
    覺四諦止息，<br/>
    稽首安隱智。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The Well Gone One was the highest,<br/>
    The sage honored among bipeds.<br/>
    Awakened to the calm of the four truths,<br/>
    I bow my head to the Peaceful Sage.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a17'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>沙門中無上，<br/>
    迴邪令入正；<br/>
    世尊施寂滅，<br/>
    稽首湛然迹。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>Unsurpassed among ascetics,<br/>
    He led the perverse to what’s right.<br/>
    The Bhagavān having been extinguished,<br/>
    I bow my head to the profound path.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a19'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>無熱無瑕郄，<br/>
    其心當寂定；<br/>
    練除諸塵穢，<br/>
    稽首無垢尊。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>Without passion and flawless,<br/>
    His mind was tranquil and settled.<br/>
    He removed the contaminants,<br/>
    So I bow my head to the Undefiled Sage.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a21'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>慧眼無限量，<br/>
    甘露滅名稱；<br/>
    希有難思議，<br/>
    稽首無等倫。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The Wisdom Eye had no limit,<br/>
    And the Immortal had power and fame.<br/>
    He was extraordinarily hard to conceive;<br/>
    I bow my head to the Unequalled One.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29a23'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>吼聲如師子，<br/>
    在林無所畏；<br/>
    降魔越四姓，<br/>
    是故𥡳首禮。」</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>Roaring like a lion,<br/>
    He lived in the forest fearlessly.<br/>
    He defeated Māra and transcended the four clans,<br/>
    Therefore, I bow my head to him.”</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29a25'>大迦葉有大威德，四辯具足，說此偈已，時，彼佛𧂐不燒自燃，諸末羅等各相謂言：「今火猛熾，焰盛難止，闍維舍利，或能<!-- [9] -->消盡，當於何所求水滅之？」</td>
    <td id='p539'>539. Mahākāśyapa possessed great majesty and had perfected the four kinds of eloquence. After saying these verses, the Buddha’s firewood spontaneously ignited. The Mallas said to each other, “Now, the fire is burning fiercely! The fire grows so much, it’ll be difficult to stop; it’ll cremate his remains until they disappear! Perhaps we should douse it with water?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29a28'>時，佛𧂐側有娑羅樹神，篤信佛道，尋以神力滅佛𧂐火。</td>
    <td id='p540'>540. Then, the spirit of the sal trees was in the firewood, and it believed in the Buddha’s path. It immediately used it’s miraculous power to douse the fire burning the Buddha’s firewood.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29a29'>時，諸末羅復相謂言：「此拘尸城左右十二由旬，所有香花，盡當採取，供佛舍利。」尋詣城側，取諸香花，以用供養。</td>
    <td id='p541'>541. The Mallas again said to each other, “There are fragrant flowers for twenty yojanas left and right of Kuśinagara. We should collect all of them and offer them to the Buddha’s remains!” They immediately went to the city and picked those fragrant flowers to use as offerings.</td>
  </tr>
<tr>
  <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29b3'></td>
  <td class='subheading'>41. Dividing the Buddha’s Remains</td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29b3'>時，波<!-- [＊] -->婆國末羅民眾，聞佛於雙樹滅度，皆自念言：「今我宜往，求舍利分，自於本土，起塔供養。」</td>
    <td id='p543'>543. The Malla people of Pāvā heard that the Buddha had parinirvāṇa-ed between a pair of trees. They thought to themselves, “Now, we ought to go and get a portion of his remains. Ours was his homeland, after all! We’ll build a shrine and give offerings to it.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29b5'>時，波<!-- [＊] -->婆國諸末羅即下國中，嚴四種兵——象兵、馬兵、車兵、步兵，到拘尸城，遣使者言：「聞佛眾祐，止此滅度，彼亦我師，敬慕之心，來請骨分，當於本國起塔供養。」</td>
    <td id='p544'>544. Those Mallas of Pāvā raised from their vassals a fourfold army consisting of elephant troops, horse troops, chariot troops, and foot troops. They went to Kuśinagara and sent an envoy. They said, “We’ve heard that the Buddha, the Bhagavān, stopped and has been extinguished. He was our teacher, too, and dear to our hearts. We are coming to request a portion of his bones. We will build a shrine in his homeland and give offerings to it.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29b9'>拘尸王答曰：「如是！如是！誠如<!-- [10] -->所言，但為世尊垂降此土，於茲滅度，國內士民，當自供養，遠勞諸君，<!-- [11] -->舍利分<!-- [12] -->不可得。」</td>
    <td id='p545'>545. The King of Kuśinagara replied, “Yes, yes! Indeed, it’s as you say. However, it was in this land that the Bhagavān fell ill, and it was here that he parinirvāṇa-ed. The country’s officials and people gave their own offerings. You gentlemen have come from far away, but you cannot have a portion of his remains.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29b12'>時，遮羅頗國諸跋離民眾，及羅摩伽國拘利民眾、毘留提國婆羅門眾、迦維羅衛國釋種民眾、毘舍離國離車民眾，及摩竭王阿闍世，<!-- [20] -->聞如來於拘尸城雙樹間而取滅度，皆自念言：「今我宜往，求舍利分。」</td>
    <td id='p546'>546. Then, an assembly of Bula people from Calakalpa, an assembly of Kraudya people from Rāmagrāma, an assembly of Brāhmaṇa people from Viṣṇudvīpa, an assembly of Śākya people from Kapilavastu, an assembly of Licchavi people from Vaiśālī, and King Ajātaśatru from Magadha heard that the Tathāgata had chosen to parinirvāṇa between a pair of trees near Kuśinagara. They thought to themselves, “Now, we ought to go and seek a portion of his remains.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29b16'>時，諸國王阿闍世等，即下國中，嚴四種兵——象兵、馬兵、車兵、步兵，進<!-- [21] -->渡恒水，即勅婆羅門<!-- [22] -->香姓：「汝持我名，入拘尸城，致問諸末羅等：『起居輕利，遊步強耶？吾於諸賢，每相宗敬，鄰境義和，曾無諍訟。我聞如來於君國內而取滅度，唯無上尊，實我所天，故從遠來，求請骨分，欲還本土，起塔供養。設與我者，舉國重寶，與君共之。』」</td>
    <td id='p547'>547. The kings of these countries like Ajātaśatru then raised from their vassals fourfold armies consisting of elephant troops, horse troops, chariot troops, and foot troops, and they advanced to cross the Gaṅgā River. They ordered the priest Dhūmragotra, “Go to Kuśinagara in my name and put this question to the Mallas: ‘Has life been easy? Have your travels been difficult? We extend every respect to you gentlemen. We’re friendly neighbors who’re never in conflict. We’ve heard that the Tathāgata chose to parinirvāṇa in your country, sir. Only the unsurpassed sage was our true deity, so we’ve traveled from far away to request a portion of his bones. We’ll take them back to our homelands and build shrines for giving offerings to them. If you were to give this to us, it would enrich the whole country, and we’d share that with you, sir.’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29b24'>時，香姓婆羅門受王教已，即詣彼城，<!-- [23] -->語諸末羅曰：「摩竭大王致問無量：『起居輕利，遊步強耶？吾於諸君，每相宗敬，鄰境義和，曾無諍訟。我聞如來於君國內而取滅度，唯無上尊，實我所天，故從遠來，求請骨分，欲還本土，起塔供養。設與我者，舉國重寶，與君共之。』」</td>
    <td id='p548'>548. Accepting the King’s instruction, the priest Dhūmragotra went to that city and said to the Mallas, “The Great King of Magadha asks immeasurably: ‘Has life been easy? Have your travels been difficult? We extend every respect to you gentlemen. We’re friendly neighbors who’re never in conflict. We’ve heard that the Tathāgata chose to parinirvāṇa in your country, sir. Only the unsurpassed sage was our true deity, so we’ve traveled from far away to request a portion of his bones. We’ll take them back to our homelands and build shrines for giving offerings to them. If you were to give this to us, it would enrich the whole country, and we’d share that with you, sir.’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c1'>時，諸末羅報香姓曰：「如是！如是！誠如君言。但為世尊埀降此土，於茲滅度，國內士民自當供養，遠勞諸君，舍利分<!-- [＊] -->不可得。」</td>
    <td id='p549'>549. The Mallas replied to Dhūmragotra, “Yes, yes! Indeed, it’s as you say, sir. However, it was in this land that the Bhagavān fell ill, and it was here that he parinirvāṇa-ed. The country’s officials and people gave their own offerings. You gentlemen have come from far away, but you cannot have a portion of his remains.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c4'>時，諸國王即集群臣，眾共立議，作<!-- [24] -->頌告曰：</td>
    <td id='p550'>550. The kings of those countries then assembled their ministers, and held a discussion. They composed these verses:</td>
  </tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29c6'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>「吾等和議，<br/>
    遠來拜首，<br/>
    遜言求分，<br/>
    如不見與，</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>“We’ve spoken peacefully,<br/>
    Having traveled far and bowed.<br/>
    We humbly asked for our portion,<br/>
    But you won’t share them.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29c8'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>四兵在此，<br/>
    不惜身命，<br/>
    義而弗獲，<br/>
    當以力取。」</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>Our fourfold army is here;<br/>
    They don’t begrudge their lives.<br/>
    If we can’t get it with reason,<br/>
    We’ll take it by force.”</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c10'>時，拘尸國即集群臣，眾共立議，以偈答曰：</td>
    <td id='p551'>551. The ministers of Kuśinagara assembled and held a discussion on the matter. They composed these verses in answer:</td>
  </tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29c11'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>「遠勞諸君，<br/>
    屈辱拜首，<br/>
    如來遺形，<br/>
    不敢相許，</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>“You gentleman have traveled far,<br/>
    And you’ve bowed in humility.<br/>
    The Tathāgata’s remains,<br/>
    We can’t bring ourselves grant you.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.29c14'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>彼欲舉兵，<br/>
    吾斯亦有，<br/>
    畢命相抵，<br/>
    未之有畏。」</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>Those armies that you’ve raised<br/>
    Are possessed by us as well.<br/>
    They’ll fight to the death<br/>
    Before they’ll have any fear.”</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c15'>時，香姓婆羅門曉眾人曰：「諸賢！長夜受佛教誡，口誦法言，心服仁化，一切眾生常念欲安，寧可<!-- [27] -->諍佛舍利共相殘<!-- [28] -->害？如來遺形<!-- [29] -->欲以廣益，舍利現在但當分<!-- [30] -->取。」</td>
    <td id='p552'>552. The priest Dhūmragotra announced to the assembly, “Gentlemen, we’ve accepted the Buddha’s instruction for so long. We’ve recited the Dharma’s words and our hearts follow his humane education. All sentient beings are constantly mindful of him and desire peace. Would they rather us fight over the Buddha’s remains and hurt each other? They would want the Tathāgata’s remains to be of broad benefit. We should simply divide his remains now.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c18'>眾咸稱善，尋復議言：「誰堪分者？」</td>
    <td id='p553'>553. The assembly all hailed this as good, and they immediately discussed this: “Who is qualified to divide them up?”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c19'>皆<!-- [31] -->言香姓婆羅門仁智<!-- [32] -->平均，可使分也。</td>
    <td id='p554'>554. They said, “The priest Dhūmragotra is a humane sage and balanced. We could have him divide them up.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c20'>時，諸國王即命香姓：「汝為我等分佛舍利，均作八分。」</td>
    <td id='p555'>555. The kings then commanded Dhūmragotra, “You will divide up the Buddha’s remains equally into eight portions.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c21'>於時，香姓聞諸王語已，即詣舍利所，頭面禮畢，徐前取佛上牙，別置一面。尋遣使者，齎佛上牙，詣阿闍世王所，</td>
    <td id='p556'>556. Dhūmragotra heard the kings say this and went to the remains. He bowed his head to them deeply, and then picked up the Buddha’s upper teeth. He divided them and put them aside. He sent someone to deliver the Buddha’s upper teeth to King Ajātaśatru.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c24'>語使者言：「汝以我聲，上白大王：『起居輕利，遊步強耶？舍利未至，傾遲無量耶？今付使者如來上牙，竝可供養，以慰企望，明星出時，分舍利訖，當自奉送。』」</td>
    <td id='p557'>557. He told the messenger, “Using my name, go up to the Great King and say, ‘Has life been easy? Have your travels been difficult? Is the wait for the Buddha’s remains taking forever? Now, I’ve dispatched this messenger to deliver the Tathāgata’s upper teeth. They are worthy of offerings. I hope that the dividing of the remains will be completed by the time the morning star rises. I will present them myself.’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.29c27'>時，彼使者受香姓語已，即詣阿闍世王所，白言：「香姓婆羅門致問無量：『起居輕利，遊步強耶？舍利未至，傾遲無量耶？今付使者如來上牙，竝可供養，以慰企望，明星出時，分舍利訖，當自奉送。』」</td>
    <td id='p558'>558. The messenger accepted Dhūmragotra’s statement and went to king Ajātaśatru. He said, “The priest Dhūmragotra has sent me to ask immeasurably, ‘Has life been easy? Have your travels been difficult? Is the wait for the Buddha’s remains taking forever? Now, I’ve dispatched this messenger to deliver the Tathāgata’s upper teeth. They are worthy of offerings. I hope that the dividing of the remains will be completed by the time the morning star rises. I will present them myself.’”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a3'>爾時，香姓以一瓶受<!-- [1] -->一石許，即分舍利，均為八分已，告眾人言：「願以此瓶，眾議見與，自欲於舍起塔供養。」</td>
    <td id='p559'>559. Dhūmragotra then received one stone [weight of remains] and divided them with a jar. Once the eight portions were equal, he told the assembled people, “Using this vase, please see that the remains are being given out fairly. You can build your own shrines to house them and give offerings to them.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a5'>皆言：「智哉！是為知時，即共聽與。」</td>
    <td id='p560'>560. They said, “That’s wise! When the time comes, we’ll permit the distribution together.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a6'>時，有畢鉢村人白眾人言：「乞地燋炭，起塔供養。」</td>
    <td id='p561'>561. Someone from the village of Pippala said to the assembled people, “I beg to have the charcoal from the ground. We’ll build a shrine and give offerings to it!”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a7'>皆言：「與之。」</td>
    <td id='p562'>562. “We’ll give it to you.”</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a8'>時，拘尸國人得舍利分，即於其土起塔供養。</td>
    <td id='p563'>563. The people of Kuśinagara received their share of the remains. They then built a shrine in their land and gave offerings to it.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a9'>波婆國人、遮羅國、羅摩伽國、毘留提國、迦維羅衛國、毘舍離國、摩竭國阿闍世王等，得舍利分已，各歸其國，起塔供養。香姓婆羅門持舍利瓶歸起塔廟，畢鉢村人持地燋炭歸起塔廟。</td>
    <td id='p564'>564. The people of Pāvā, Calakalpa, Rāmagrāma, Viṣṇudvīpa, Kapilavastu, Vaiśālī, and King Ajātaśatru of Magadha received their shares of the remains. They each returned to their country, built a shrine, and gave offerings to it. The priest Dhūmragotra brought the vase for building a shrine. The person from Pippala took their portion to their land. They built a shrine to the charcoal of the cremation.</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a13'>當於爾時，如來舍利起於八塔，第九瓶塔，第十炭塔，第十一生時髮塔。</td>
    <td id='p565'>565. At the time, eight shrines were built for the Tathāgata’s remains, a ninth shrine for the vase, a tenth shrine for the charcoal, and an eleventh shrine for hair from the Buddha’s birth.</td>
  </tr>
<tr>
  <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a15'></td>
  <td class='subheading'>42. Final Eulogy</td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30a15'>何等時佛生？<!-- [4] -->何等時成道？何等時滅度？<!-- [5] -->沸星出時生，沸星出出家，沸星出成道，沸星出滅度<!-- [6] -->(丹本注云問中應有何等時出家諸本並闕)。</td>
    <td id='p567'>567. On what day was the Buddha born? On what day did he achieved awakening? On what day did he parinirvāṇa? He was born when the star Puṣya rose. He left home when the star Puṣya rose. He achieved awakening when the star Puṣya rose. He parinirvāṇa-ed when the star Puṣya rose.</td>
  </tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30a18'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>何等生二足尊？<br/>
    何等出叢林苦？<br/>
    何等得最上道？<br/>
    何等入涅槃城？</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>When was the sage of bipeds born?<br/>
    When did he escape the jungle of suffering?<br/>
    When did he find the unsurpassed path?<br/>
    When did he enter the city of nirvāṇa?</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30a20'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>沸星生二足尊，<br/>
    沸星出叢林苦，<br/>
    沸星得最上道，<br/>
    沸星入涅槃城。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The sage of bipeds was born when Puṣya rose.<br/>
    He escaped the jungle when Puṣya rose.<br/>
    He found the unsurpassed path when Puṣya rose.<br/>
    He entered the city of <em>nirvāṇa</em> when Puṣya rose.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30a22'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>八日如來生，<br/>
    八日佛出家，<br/>
    八日成菩提，<br/>
    八日取滅度。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>On the eighth day, the Tathāgata was born.<br/>
    On the eighth day, the Buddha left home.<br/>
    On the eighth day, he achieved awakening.<br/>
    On the eighth day, he chose to parinirvāṇa.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30a24'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>八日生二足尊，<br/>
    八日出叢林苦，<br/>
    八日成最上道，<br/>
    八日入泥洹城。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>On the eighth day, the sage of bipeds was born.<br/>
    On the eighth day, he escaped the jungle.<br/>
    On the eighth day, he found the unsurpassed path.<br/>
    On the eighth day, he entered the city of nirvāṇa.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30a26'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>二月如來生，<br/>
    二月佛出家，<br/>
    二月成菩提，<br/>
    八日取涅槃。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>In the second month, the Tathāgata was born.<br/>
    In the second month, the Buddha left home.<br/>
    In the second month, he achieved awakening.<br/>
    On the eighth day, he chose to parinirvāṇa.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30a28'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>二月生二足尊，<br/>
    二月出叢林苦，<br/>
    二月得最上道，<br/>
    八日入涅槃城。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>In the second month, the sage of bipeds was born.<br/>
    In the second month, he escaped the jungle.<br/>
    In the second month, he found the unsurpassed path.<br/>
    On the eighth day, he entered the city of nirvāṇa.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30b1'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>娑羅花熾盛，<br/>
    種種光相照；<br/>
    於其本生處，<br/>
    如來取滅度。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The sal flowers were blooming,<br/>
    Shining with a all sorts of light.<br/>
    In his original birthplace,<br/>
    The Tathāgata chose to parinirvāṇa.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
  <td title='t1.1.30b3'><ul class='verse'>
    <li class='ch'>大慈般涅槃，<br/>
    多人稱讚禮；<br/>
    盡度諸恐畏，<br/>
    決定取滅度。</li>
  </ul></td>
  <td><ul class='verse'>
    <li>The Great Compassionate One’s parinirvāṇa<br/>
    Is praised and honored by many people.<br/>
    Having entirely forded terrible things,<br/>
    He decided to parinirvāṇa.</li>
  </ul></td>
</tr>
  <tr>
    <td class='ch' title='t1.1.30b5'>佛說長阿含經卷第四</td>
    <td class="fasc_heading"></td>
  </tr>
</table>

<hr/>

<h3 id="notes">Notes</h3>

<ol class="notes-list">
<li id="n1"><p><em>The Journey</em>. C. <span class="ch">遊行</span>, S. <em>carati</em> or <em>viharati</em>?</p>
<p>This title is unique to the Dharmaguptaka version of the <em>Parinirvāṇa Sūtra</em>. The term <span class="ch">遊行</span> means “to wander, journey, roam.” It must refer to the Buddha’s final tour that ended with his passing away on the road to Kuśinagara. I had previously translated this title as “the final journey,” addeding “final” to give it more context. I’ve removed that addition for the sake of accuracy. It’s unclear what the original Indic title might have been, but S. <em>carati</em> or <em>viharati</em> would be the most straightforward possibilities. [<a href="#ref1">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n2"><p>This is <em>sūtra</em> no. 2 of the <cite>Dīrgha Āgama</cite> (<a href="https://cbetaonline.dila.edu.tw/zh/T01n0001_p0011a07" target="_blank">T1.1.11a7-30b4</a>). Its direct parallels are: <a href="https://suttacentral.net/dn16" target="_blank">DN 16</a>, <a href="https://cbetaonline.dila.edu.tw/zh/T0005_001" target="_blank">T5</a>, <a href="https://cbetaonline.dila.edu.tw/zh/T0006_001" target="_blank">T6</a>, <a href="https://cbetaonline.dila.edu.tw/zh/T0007_001" target="_blank">T7</a>, a portion of <a href="https://cbetaonline.dila.edu.tw/zh/T24n1451_p0382b29" target="_blank">T1451</a> from the Chinese <cite>Mūlasarvāstivāda Vinaya</cite>, a portion of the Tibetan translation of the <cite>Mūlasarvāstivāda Vinaya</cite>, and a <a href="https://suttacentral.net/sf245/san/waldschmidt" target="_blank">Sanskrit manuscript</a> produced by Waldschmidt from Central Asian manuscripts.</p>
<p>There are also numerous parallels to portions of this <em>sūtra</em> that appear elsewhere in various early Buddhist sources. The lengthiest of these is <a href="https://suttacentral.net/dn17" target="_blank">DN 17</a>, which is embedded in its entirety in this version of the <em>sūtra</em>. I will cover these parallels where they are relevant in the notes below. [<a href="#ref2">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n3"><p>This first section has at least seven parallels in other Buddhist sources that I have located. All but one of the other versions of the <cite>Parinirvāṇa Sūtra</cite> begin with this story. The exception is Taisho No. 7 (which begins the narrative with the Buddha and Ānanda visiting Cāpāla Shrine). In addition to these direct parallels, this story also existed as an independent <em>sūtra</em> which has (at least) four surviving witnesses: <a href="https://suttacentral.net/an7.22" target="_blank">AN 7.22</a>, <a href="../ekottarika/40/EA_40_02.html" target="_blank">EĀ 40.2</a>, <a href="../madhyama/MA_142.html" target="_blank">MĀ 142</a>, and <a href="../../02_avadana/dharmapada/T211/T211c36v1.html" target="_blank">Chapter 36 of the <cite>Dharmapāda Avadāna</cite></a>.</p>
<p>T7 represents a somewhat different version of the <cite>Parinirvāṇa Sūtra</cite>. While it lacks this story about Ajātaśatru, it does include the seven ways of remaining unconquerable. In its version of events, the Buddha teaches these seven practices to the Licchavis after he announces his impending entry into <em>nirvāṇa</em>. His advice to them serves as a consolation, and that consolation is then extended to the monks, too, in the form of a discourse on seven ways to keep the Dharma from declining.</p>
<p>In these other versions of the <em>sūtra</em>, the story of Ajātaśatru dispatching his minister to ask for the Buddha’s advice surely represented the Buddhist response to the imperial ascent of Magadha that began with Ajātaśatru’s rule. Historians generally accept that Ajātaśatru rose to power through a <em>coup d’état</em> in the last decade of the Buddha’s life, as these events are corroborated by other Indian sources. Ajātaśatru afterward launched wars of conquest on the lands surrounding Magadha around the time of or after the Buddha’s <em>parinirvāṇa</em>.</p>
<p>Here, Ajātaśatru is depicted as planning his invasion of the Vṛji (P. Vajjī) tribal confederacy to the north. The Śākya tribe was culturally much closer to the Vṛji tribes than they were to Magadha, and so the Buddha’s response to Ajātaśatru’s request for advice isn’t unexpected. The generation of Buddhists who experienced the wars that took place would have had no love of the usurper, given that a large portion of the Saṅgha were likely Śākyans.</p>
<p>Despite the Buddha’s reassurances, it appears that Ajātaśatru did prevail over the Vṛji and established Pāṭaliputra as a stronghold in their territory. The commentarial tradition maintained by Theravādins tells a story of Vassakāra taking the Buddha’s advice to heart and fomenting internal divisions between the Vṛji tribes to weaken them. [<a href="#ref3">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n4"><p><em>Magadha</em>. C. <span class="ch">摩竭</span> (EMC. mua-gɪɛt = <em>maga[dha]</em>), G. ?, P. <em>magadha</em>, S. <em>magadha</em>. This was one of the major Indian kingdoms during the Buddha’s lifetime. It was situated west of Anga, east of Kaśi, and south of the Vṛji confederacy on the Gangetic Plain of northeast India.</p>
<p><em>Ajātaśatru</em>. C. <span class="ch">阿闍世</span> (EMC. •a-ʒɪă-ʃɪɛi = <em>aja’śa[tru]</em>), G. <em>*ajadaśatru</em>, P. <em>ajātasattu</em>, S. <em>ajātaśatru</em>. The C. is an abbreviated transliteration. This name is actually a pejorative epithet (lit. “unborn enemy”) that referred to the fact that he would overthrow his father Bimbisāra to usurp the throne. Jain sources call him Kūṇika or Kūṇiya, and another Buddhist tradition preserved in the <cite>Ekottarika Āgama</cite> knew him as Pararuci (lit. “exceeding joy”). He was a usurper king of Magadha who seized the throne toward the end of the Buddha’s life. He would later begin the formation of the first Indian empire that would reach its height during the reign of King Aśoka in the 3rd century BCE.</p>
<p><em>Vṛji</em>. C. <span class="ch">跋祇</span> (EMC. buat-giĕ = <em>vaji</em>), G. <em>vaji</em>, P. <em>vajjī</em>, S. <em>vṛji</em>. This was the territory of a tribal confederacy that included the Licchavi and Videha tribes mentioned frequently in Buddhist sources. They occupied the lands between Magadha and the Himalaya mountains. Their most prominent city was Vaiśālī. [<a href="#ref4">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n5"><p><em>Varṣākāra</em>. C. <span class="ch">禹舍</span> (EMC. ɦɪu-ʃɪă = Pr. <em>vaṣa[kara]</em>), G. <em>vaṣakara</em>?, P. <em>vassakāra</em>, S. <em>varṣākāra</em>. The Chinese transliteration abbreviates his name to simply <em>vaṣa</em>, which is the G. equivalent of P. <em>vassa</em> and S. <em>varṣā</em>. According to the Theravāda commentarial tradition, he was the prime minister of Magadha during Bimbisāra’s reign and continued in that position under Ajātaśatru. For more of his background, cf. his entry in the <cite><a href="https://www.palikanon.com/english/pali_names/va/vassakaara.htm" target="_blank">Buddhist Dictionary of Pali Proper Names</a></cite>. [<a href="#ref5">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n6"><p>One of the difference between the parallels of this story is whether or not the Buddha addresses Varṣākāra when giving this teaching. The insertion of Ānanda into the conversation happens in most of the well-developed versions of the story. However, the succinct version found in the Chinese <em>Dharmapāda Avadāna</em> does not involve Ānanda at all. Instead, the Buddha has a conversation with Varṣākāra as he normally would.</p>
<p>The opposite extreme is found here in DĀ 2 and EĀ 40.2 where the Buddha speaks only to Ānanda. The EĀ version explicitly tells us that this was because Varṣākāra was not dressed properly to receive a teaching from the Buddha.</p>
<p>DN 16 does not depict the Buddha speaking to Vassakāra about the seven practices, but he does address the brahmin when he mentions having taught the seven practices to the Vajjians in the past. This part of the story is not found here in DĀ 2 or in the shorter versions.</p>
<p>The Sarvāstivāda parallels depict the Buddha asking Ānanda about each practice and then addressing Varṣākāra with his conclusion about it, and they also include the Buddha telling the brahmin about having taught the Vṛji these practices in the past.</p>
<p>It would seem that DN 16 has borrowed from the Sarvāstivāda version of the story, especially given how close the descriptions of the practices themselves are to those parallels compared to DĀ 2 and the others. [<a href="#ref6">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n7"><p>The seven practices that follow are noticeably different in wording from the list we find in DN 16. The same basic meanings and themes are evident, however. The item that has the most difference is the one about the treatment of women. Here in DĀ 2, it is almost unrecognizable as a commentary on relations between men and women. In the context of the other parallels, however, it probably should be taken as a statement about the behavior of husbands toward their wives. [<a href="#ref7">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n8"><p><em>they’ll prosper even more</em>. C. <span class="ch">轉更增上</span>. I’ve adopted the variant reading that changes <span class="ch">增上</span> to <span class="ch">增盛</span>. The Taisho reading is inconsistent with the other standard passages in this list of practices. It was likely a typo by a copyist. <span class="ch">增上</span> is repeated in the next item’s conclusion, and then the text returns to <span class="ch">增盛</span>. [<a href="#ref8">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n9"><p>This first set of seven practices that ensures the growth of the teaching appears to be the older part of this story about the Buddha giving the monks advice for the future. That would be expected considering that it directly parallels the advice that he had been given to the Vṛji.</p>
<p>When we compare the additional lists that follow this one in the various parallels, there is a great deal of variability, suggesting that they were added over time in each tradition. The difference between this version and DN 16 is that two lists have been added: The seven things to esteem and the six recollections. The addition of the six recollections is unique to this version and certainly appears to be added by the Dharmaguptakas. [<a href="#ref9">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n10"><p><em>the Ajapāla Nigrodha tree on the bank of the Nairañjanā River in Uruvilvā</em>. C. <span class="ch">鬱鞞羅、尼連禪水邊，阿遊波陀、尼俱律樹</span>, P. <em>uruvelāyaṁ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre ajapālanigrodhe</em>. The C. transliterations of these placenames generally agree with DN 21, with some of the usual differences in pronunciation found in other Prakrit languages.</p>
<p><em></em>. C. <span class="ch">阿遊波陀</span> (EMC. •a-yiəu-pua-da = <em>ayopada</em>?), P. <em>ajapāla</em>. In many G. texts, <em>j</em> > <em>y</em> and <em>l</em> > <em>d</em> when compared to Sanskrit or Pali, so this variation in pronunciation here is not unexpected. The word is interpreted to mean a goatherd in P. sources (i.e., <em>aja</em> + <em>pāla</em> = "goat protector"), but there is no attestation in G. that I have found to confirm the same reading here. I have adopted the S. reading as per my usual policy.</p>
<p><em>banyan tree</em>. C. <span class="ch">尼俱律樹</span> (EMC. ṇɪi-kɪu-lɪuĕt + “tree” = nigro[dha]), P. <em>nigrodha</em>, S. <em>nyagrodha</em>. Here, <span class="ch">俱律</span> represent the syllable <em>-kro-</em>, and the final syllable has been dropped.</p>
<p><em>beside the Nairañjanā River</em>. C. <span class="ch">尼連禪水邊</span> (EMC. ṇɪi-lɪɛn-ʒɪɛn + “riverside” = niranjan[a]?), P. <em>nerañjarā</em>, S. <em>nairañjanā</em>. The translit. here matches the S. better than it does the P.</p>
<p><em>Uruvilvā</em>. C. <span class="ch">鬱鞞羅</span> (EMC. •ɪuət-bei-la = [ur]uvela?), P. <em>uruvelā</em>, S. <em>uruvilvā</em>. Lacking a G. or other Prakrit attestation, I can only guess that the initial <em>ur-</em> has been omitted from the translit. here. [<a href="#ref10">back</a>]</p></li>
<li id="n11"><p><em>Fragrance Shrine</em>. C. <span class="ch">香塔</span>. The Indic original is unclear, but it must have meant something like “fragrance” or “incense.” In Pali, the place where the Buddha announced he would enter nirvāṇa in three months was a gabled hall in the Great Forest (P. <em>mahāvanaṁ kūṭāgārasālā</em>) near P. Vesālī. [<a href="#ref11">back</a>]</p></li>
</ol>
<hr/>
<p class="translator">Translator: Charles Patton</p>
